# Proposed OEIS comments: A250791 to A251652

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A250791

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250791>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10547](../papers/10501-11000/10547-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) - 3*a(n-2) - 2*a(n-3) + 2*a(n-4).
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Nov 20 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A250793

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250793>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [9557](../papers/09501-10000/09557-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 8*a(n-1) - 20*a(n-2) + 8*a(n-3) + 33*a(n-4) - 36*a(n-5) + 8*a(n-7).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(59 - 284*x + 271*x^2 + 416*x^3 - 624*x^4 + 10*x^5 + 128*x^6) / ((1 - x)^2*(1 - 2*x)*(1 - 2*x - x^2)*(1 - 2*x - 4*x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 20 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 32 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A250806

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250806>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4118](../papers/04001-04500/04118-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 6*a(n-1) - 11*a(n-2) + 6*a(n-3); a(n) = (126*3^n - 99*2^n + 20)/2.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(100 - 221*x + 141*x^2) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 2*x)*(1 - 3*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 20 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 27 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical: a(n) = (1695/4)*n^4 + 3786*n^3 + (54287/4)*n^2 + (33539/2)*n + 6561.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Nov 21 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 6 561 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A250837

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250837>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [520](../papers/00501-01000/00520-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 70 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 243 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 70, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical: a(n) = 10*a(n-1) - 35*a(n-2) + 50*a(n-3) - 24*a(n-4); a(n) = (1168*4^n - 1026*3^n + 216*2^n + 8)/3.
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(339 - 1669*x + 2786*x^2 - 1464*x^3) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 2*x)*(1 - 3*x)*(1 - 4*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 22 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 256 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A250923

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250923>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [486](../papers/00001-00500/00486-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 81 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 243 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 81, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [10802](../papers/10501-11000/10802-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 15*a(n-1) - 24*a(n-2) - 8*a(n-3).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(209 - 391*x - 128*x^2) / (1 - 15*x + 24*x^2 + 8*x^3). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 24 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 16 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A251040

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A251040>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [5039](../papers/05001-05500/05039-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 60 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 176 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 60, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A251207

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A251207>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [533](../papers/00501-01000/00533-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 65 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 729 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 65, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A251217

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A251217>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [548](../papers/00501-01000/00548-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 62 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 128 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 62, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A251257

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A251257>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [590](../papers/00501-01000/00590-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 49 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 49, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A251296

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A251296>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [624](../papers/00501-01000/00624-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 44 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 44, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A251397

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A251397>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [484](../papers/00001-00500/00484-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 81 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 81, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 16 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 1. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
